. Such a DTR is feasible in
{\cal G}
. Then, the optimized social
welfare given
\mathbf{b}
is

\displaystyle W\left(g_{\mathbf{b}}^{\ast}\right)=\frac{1}{v_{1:T}}\delta\sum_{j=1}^{v_{1:T}}b_{j}.

Let
\hat{g}=\left(\hat{g}_{1},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T}\right):{\cal H}_{1}\times\cdots\times{\cal H}_{T}\mapsto\left\{0,1\right\}^{T}

be an arbitrary DTR depending on the sample
\left(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\right)
,
and let
\hat{\mathbf{b}}\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{v_{1:T}}
be a
binary vector such that its
j
-th element is given by

\displaystyle\hat{b}_{j}=1\left\{\hat{g}_{1}\left(h_{1j}\right)=d_{1j},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T}\left(h_{Tj}\right)=d_{Tj}\right\}.

We define by
g\left(\mathbf{b}\right)
a prior of
\mathbf{b}

such that
b_{1},\ldots,b_{v_{1:T}}
are i.i.d and
b_{1}\sim\mbox{Ber}\left(1/2\right)
.

Then the maximum average welfare regret on
\mathcal{P}\left(\mathbf{1}_{T},\kappa\right)
satisfies the following:

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}\left(\mathbf{1}_{T},\kappa\right)}E_{P^{n}}\left[W_{{\cal G}}^{\ast}-W\left(\hat{g}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ \sup_{P_{\mathbf{b}}\in{\cal P}^{\ast}}E_{P_{\mathbf{b}}^{n}}\left[W\left(g_{\mathbf{b}}^{\ast}\right)-W\left(\hat{g}\right)\right]\geq\int_{\mathbf{b}}E_{P_{\mathbf{b}}^{n}}\left[W\left(g_{\mathbf{b}}^{\ast}\right)-W\left(\hat{g}\right)\right]dg(\mathbf{b})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ \delta\int_{\mathbf{b}}\int_{Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}}P_{\tilde{Z}}\left(\left\{b(\tilde{Z})\neq\hat{b}(\tilde{Z})\right\}\right)dP_{\mathbf{b}}^{n}\left(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\right)dg(\mathbf{b})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ \inf_{\hat{g}\in\mathcal{G}}\delta\int_{\mathbf{b}}\int_{Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}}P_{\tilde{Z}}\left(\left\{b(\tilde{Z})\neq\hat{b}(\tilde{Z})\right\}\right)dP_{\mathbf{b}}^{n}\left(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\right)dg(\mathbf{b}),

where
P_{\tilde{Z}}
is a probability measure of
\tilde{Z}
, and

b(\tilde{Z})
and
\hat{b}(\tilde{Z})
are
elements of
\mathbf{b}
and
\hat{\mathbf{b}}
such
that
b\left(\tilde{z}_{j}\right)=b_{j}
and
\hat{b}\left(\tilde{z}_{j}\right)=\hat{b}_{j}
, respectively.
Note that the above minimization problem can be seen as the minimization of the Bayes risk when the loss function corresponds to the classification error for predicting the binary random variable
b(\tilde{Z})
.
Hence, the risk is minimized by the Bayes classifier such that for
each
j=1,\ldots,J
,

\displaystyle\hat{b}^{\ast}\left(\tilde{z}_{j}\right)=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{c}\mbox{if }g\left(b_{j}=1\mid Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\right)\geq 1/2\\
\mbox{otherwise}\end{array}\end{cases},

where
g\left(b_{j}=1\mid Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\right)
is the posterior distribution for
b_{j}=1
. This Bayes
classifier is achieved by a DTR
\hat{g}^{\ast}\equiv\left(\hat{g}_{1}^{\ast},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T}^{\ast}\right)

that satisfies for
j=1,\ldots,j
,

\displaystyle\left(\hat{g}_{1}^{\ast}\left(h_{1j}\right),\ldots,\hat{g}_{T}^{\ast}\left(h_{Tj}\right)\right)=\begin{cases}\begin{array}[]{c}\text{$\underline{d}$}_{Tj}\\
\left(1-d_{1j},\ldots,1-d_{Tj}\right)\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{c}\mbox{if }g\left(b_{j}=1\mid Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\right)\geq 1/2\\
\mbox{otherwise}\end{array}\end{cases}.

Note that
\hat{g}^{\ast}
is feasible in
\mathcal{G}
.

Then, using
\hat{g}^{\ast}
, the minimized risk is given by

\displaystyle\delta\int_{Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}}E_{\tilde{Z}}\left[\min\left\{g\left(b\left(\tilde{Z}\right)=1\mid Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\right),1-g\left(b\left(\tilde{Z}\right)=1\mid Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\right)\right\}\right]d\tilde{P}^{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{v_{1:T}}\delta\int_{Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{v_{1:T}}\left[\min\left\{g\left(b_{j}=1\mid Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\right),1-g\left(b_{j}=1\mid Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\right)\right\}\right]d\tilde{P}^{n},

(33)

where
\tilde{P}
is the marginal likelihood of
\left\{\left\{Y_{it}\left(\text{$\underline{d}$}_{t}\right)\right\}_{\text{$\underline{d}$}_{t}\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{t}},\left\{D_{it},X_{it}\right\}\right\}_{i=1,\ldots,n;t=1,\ldots,T}

with prior
g(\mathbf{b})
.

For each
j=1,\ldots,v_{1:T}
, let

\displaystyle k_{j}^{+}

\displaystyle=\#\left\{i:\tilde{Z}_{i}=\tilde{z}_{j},Y_{iT}=\frac{1}{2}\right\},

\displaystyle k_{j}^{-}

\displaystyle=\#\left\{i:\tilde{Z}_{i}=\tilde{z}_{j},Y_{iT}=-\frac{1}{2}\right\}.

Then the posteriors for
b_{j}=1
can be written as

\displaystyle g\left(b_{j}=1\mid Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\right)=\begin{cases}\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{1}{2}\\
\frac{\left(\frac{1}{2}+\delta\right)^{k_{j}^{+}}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\delta\right)^{k_{j}^{-}}}{\left(\frac{1}{2}+\delta\right)^{k_{j}^{+}}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\delta\right)^{k_{j}^{-}}+\left(\frac{1}{2}+\delta\right)^{k_{j}^{-}}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\delta\right)^{k_{j}^{+}}}\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{c}\mbox{if }k_{j}^{+}+k_{j}^{-}=0\\
\mbox{otherwise}.\end{array}\end{cases}

Hence, the following holds:

\displaystyle\min\left\{g\left(b_{j}=1\mid Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\right),1-g\left(b_{j}=1\mid Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\frac{\min\left\{\left(\frac{1}{2}+\delta\right)^{k_{j}^{+}}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\delta\right)^{k_{j}^{-}},\left(\frac{1}{2}+\delta\right)^{k_{j}^{-}}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\delta\right)^{k_{j}^{+}}\right\}}{\left(\frac{1}{2}+\delta\right)^{k_{j}^{+}}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\delta\right)^{k_{j}^{-}}+\left(\frac{1}{2}+\delta\right)^{k_{j}^{-}}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\delta\right)^{k_{j}^{+}}}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\frac{\min\left\{1,\left(\frac{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}{\frac{1}{2}-\delta}\right)^{k_{j}^{+}-k_{j}^{-}}\right\}}{1+\left(\frac{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}{\frac{1}{2}-\delta}\right)^{k_{j}^{+}-k_{j}^{-}}}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+a^{\left|k_{j}^{+}-k_{j}^{-}\right|}},\mbox{ where }a=\frac{1+2\delta}{1-2\delta}>1.

(34)

Since

\displaystyle k_{j}^{+}-k_{j}^{-}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i:\tilde{Z}_{i}=\tilde{z}_{j}}2Y_{Ti},

plugging (
34
) into (
33
)
yields

\displaystyle(\ref{eq:proof-lower bound-1})=\frac{1}{v_{1:T}}\delta\sum_{j=1}^{v_{1:T}}E_{\tilde{P}^{n}}\left[\frac{1}{1+a^{\left|\sum_{i:\tilde{Z}_{i}=\tilde{z}_{j}}2Y_{iT}\right|}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\delta}{2v_{1:T}}\sum_{j=1}^{v_{1:T}}E_{\tilde{P}^{n}}\left[\frac{1}{a^{\left|\sum_{i:\tilde{Z}_{i}=\tilde{z}_{j}}2Y_{iT}\right|}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\delta}{2v_{1:T}}\sum_{j=1}^{v_{1:T}}a^{-E_{\tilde{P}^{n}}\left|\sum_{i:\tilde{Z}_{i}=\tilde{z}_{j}}2Y_{iT}\right|},

where
E_{\tilde{P}^{n}}\left[\cdot\right]
is the expectation with
respect to the marginal likelihood of

\displaystyle\left\{\left\{Y_{it}\left(\text{$\underline{d}$}_{t}\right)\right\}_{\text{$\underline{d}$}_{t}\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{t}},D_{it},X_{it}\right\}_{i=1,\ldots,n;t=1,\ldots,T}.

The first inequality follows by
a>1
, and the second inequality
follows by Jensen’s inequality. Given our prior distribution for
\mathbf{b}
,
for each
\text{$\underline{d}$}_{T}\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{T}
,
the marginal distribution of
Y_{iT}\left(\underline{d}_{T}\right)
is

P\left(Y_{iT}\left(\text{$\underline{d}$}_{T}\right)=1/2\right)=P\left(Y_{iT}\left(\text{$\underline{d}$}_{T}\right)=-1/2\right)=1/2

if there exist
\text{$\underline{d}$}_{Tj}
among
\text{$\underline{d}$}_{T1},\ldots,\text{$\underline{d}$}_{Tv_{1:T}}

such that
\text{$\underline{d}$}_{Tj}=\text{$\underline{d}$}_{T}
;
otherwise,
P\left(Y_{iT}\left(\text{$\underline{d}$}_{T}\right)=0\right)=1
.
Thus, we have

\displaystyle E_{\tilde{P}^{n}}\left|\sum_{i:\tilde{Z}_{i}=\tilde{z}_{j}}2Y_{iT}\right|

\displaystyle=E_{\tilde{P}^{n}}\left|\sum_{i:\tilde{Z}_{i}=\tilde{z}_{j}}2Y_{iT}\left(\text{$\underline{d}$}_{Tj}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}n\\
k\end{array}\right)\left(\frac{1}{v_{1:T}}\right)^{k}\left(1-\frac{1}{v_{1:T}}\right)^{n-k}E\left|B\left(k,\frac{1}{2}\right)-\frac{k}{2}\right|,

where
B\left(k,1/2\right)
is the binomial random variable with parameters
k
and
1/2
. By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, it follows
that

\displaystyle E\left|B\left(k,\frac{1}{2}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\right|\leq\sqrt{E\left(B\left(k,\frac{1}{2}\right)-\frac{k}{2}\right)^{2}}=\sqrt{\frac{k}{4}}.

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle E_{\tilde{P}^{n}}\left|\sum_{i:\tilde{Z}_{i}=\tilde{z}_{j}}2Y_{iT}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}n\\
k\end{array}\right)\left(\frac{1}{v_{1:T}}\right)^{k}\left(1-\frac{1}{v_{1:T}}\right)^{n-k}\sqrt{\frac{k}{4}}

\displaystyle=E\sqrt{\frac{B\left(n,\frac{1}{v_{1:T}}\right)}{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{n}{4v_{1:T}}},

where the last inequality follows by Jensen’s inequality. Hence, the
Bayes risk is bounded from below by

\displaystyle\frac{\delta}{2}a^{-\sqrt{\frac{n}{4v_{1:T}}}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\delta}{2}\exp\left\{-\left(a-1\right)\sqrt{\frac{n}{4v_{1:T}}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{2}\exp\left\{-\frac{4\delta}{1-2\delta}\sqrt{\frac{n}{4v_{1:T}}}\right\},

where the inequality follows from the fact that
1+x\leq e^{x}
for any
x
.
This lower bound on the Bayes risk has the slowest convergence rate
when
\delta
is set to be proportional to
n^{-1/2}
. Specifically,
letting
\delta=\sqrt{v_{1:T}/n}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\delta}{2}\exp\left\{-\frac{4\delta}{1-2\delta}\sqrt{\frac{n}{4v_{1:T}}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{v_{1:T}}{n}}\exp\left\{-\frac{2}{1-2\delta}\right\}\geq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{v_{1:T}}{n}}\exp\left(-4\right)\mbox{ if }1-2\delta\geq\frac{1}{2}.

The condition
1-2\delta\geq 1/2
is equivalent to
n\geq 16v_{1:T}
.
Multiplying the lower bound by
M_{T}
gives

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in{\cal P}\left(M,\kappa,\mathcal{G}\right)}E_{P^{n}}\left[W_{{\cal G}}^{\ast}-W\left(\hat{g}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\exp\left(-4\right)M_{T}\sqrt{\frac{v_{1:T}}{n}}

for all
n\geq 16v_{1:T}
.

The proof is valid irrespective of whether Assumption
3.1
holds for a pair
(P,\mathcal{G})
with any
P\in\mathcal{P}(M,\kappa,\mathcal{G})
or not.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.7
.

The result immediately follows by setting

\displaystyle t=\underset{s\in\left\{1,\ldots,T\right\}}{\mathop{\rm arg\penalty\ max}\limits}\gamma_{s}M_{s}\sqrt{\frac{v_{1:s}}{n}}

in the statement of Lemma
F.4
.
∎

F.3
Proof of Theorem
5.1

We derive uniform upper bounds on the worst-case average welfare regrets of the two DEWM methods in the case where estimated propensity scores are used instead of true ones.

Proof of Theorem
5.1
(i).

Let
P\in\mathcal{P}_{e}\cap\mathcal{P}(M,\kappa,\mathcal{G})
be fixed. Define
\hat{W}_{nt}(\text{$\underline{g}$}_{t})\equiv n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{w}_{t}^{S}(Z_{i},\text{$\underline{g}$}_{t})
and
\hat{W}_{n}\left(g\right)\equiv\sum_{t=1}^{T}\hat{W}_{nt}(\text{$\underline{g}$}_{t})
, which are estimators of
W_{t}(\text{$\underline{g}$}_{t})

and
W\left(g\right)
, respectively. It follows for any
g\in{\cal G}

that

\displaystyle W\left(g\right)-W\left(\hat{g}_{e}^{S}\right)

\displaystyle\leq W_{n}\left(g\right)-\hat{W}_{n}\left(g\right)-W_{n}\left(\hat{g}_{e}^{S}\right)+\hat{W}_{n}\left(\hat{g}_{e}^{S}\right)

\displaystyle+W\left(g\right)-W\left(\hat{g}_{e}^{S}\right)+W_{n}\left(\hat{g}_{e}^{S}\right)-W_{n}\left(g\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{\text{$\underline{d}$}_{t}\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{t}}\left[\left(\frac{\gamma_{t}Y_{it}\cdot 1\left\{\text{$\underline{D}$}_{it}=\text{$\underline{d}$}_{t}\right\}}{\prod_{s=1}^{t}e_{s}\left(d_{s},H_{is}\right)}-\frac{\gamma_{t}Y_{it}\cdot 1\left\{\text{$\underline{D}$}_{it}=\text{$\underline{d}$}_{t}\right\}}{\prod_{s=1}^{t}\hat{e}_{s}\left(d_{s},H_{is}\right)}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\times\left(\prod_{s=1}^{t}1\left\{g_{s}\left(H_{is}\right)=d_{s}\right\}-\prod_{s=1}^{t}1\left\{\hat{g}_{e,s}^{S}\left(H_{is}\right)=d_{s}\right\}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+W\left(g\right)-W_{n}\left(g\right)+W_{n}\left(\hat{g}_{e}^{S}\right)-W\left(\hat{g}_{e}^{S}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{\text{$\underline{d}$}_{t}\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{t}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\tau_{t}\left(\underline{d}_{t},H_{it}\right)-\hat{\tau}_{t}\left(\underline{d}_{t},H_{it}\right)\right|\right)+2\sup_{g\in{\cal G}}\left|W_{n}\left(g\right)-W\left(g\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{\text{$\underline{d}$}_{t}\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{t}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\tau_{t}\left(\underline{d}_{t},H_{it}\right)-\hat{\tau}_{t}\left(\underline{d}_{t},H_{it}\right)\right|\right)

\displaystyle+2\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sup_{\underline{g}_{t}\in{\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\cdots\times\mathcal{G}_{t}}}\left|W_{nt}(\text{$\underline{g}$}_{t})-W_{t}(\text{$\underline{g}$}_{t})\right|.

The first inequality follows from the fact that
\hat{g}_{e}^{S}

maximizes
\hat{W}_{n}\left(\cdot\right)
over
{\cal G}
. The second inequality follows from the fact that

\displaystyle\left|\prod_{s=1}^{t}1\left\{g_{s}\left(H_{is}\right)=d_{s}\right\}-\prod_{s=1}^{t}1\left\{\hat{g}_{e,s}^{S}\left(H_{is}\right)=d_{s}\right\}\right|\leq 1.

Thus, the average welfare regret can be bounded from above by

\displaystyle E_{P^{n}}\left[W_{{\cal G}}^{\ast}-W\left(\hat{g}_{e}^{S}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{\text{$\underline{d}$}_{t}\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{t}}E_{P^{n}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\tau_{t}\left(\text{$\underline{d}$}_{t},H_{it}\right)-\hat{\tau}_{t}\left(\text{$\underline{d}$}_{t},H_{it}\right)\right|\right]

\displaystyle+2\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sup_{\underline{g}_{t}\in{\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\cdots\times\mathcal{G}_{t}}}E_{P^{n}}\left[\left|W_{nt}(\text{$\underline{g}$}_{t})-W_{t}(\text{$\underline{g}$}_{t})\right|\right].

Therefore, by the same argument as in the proof of Theorem
3.6
(i) and from Assumption
5.1
(i), the
average welfare regret is bounded from above as

\displaystyle E_{P^{n}}\left[W_{{\cal G}}^{\ast}-W\left(\hat{g}_{e}^{S}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\frac{\gamma_{t}M_{t}}{\prod_{s=1}^{t}\kappa_{s}}\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{s=1}^{t}v_{s}}{n}}\right\}+O\left(\phi_{n}^{-1}\right).

Since this upper bound does not depend on
P\in{\cal P}_{e}\bigcap{\cal P}\left(M,\kappa,\mathcal{G}\right)
,
the upper bound is uniform over
{\cal P}_{e}\bigcap{\cal P}\left(M,\kappa,\mathcal{G}\right)
.
∎

Before proceeding to the proof of Theorem
5.1
(ii), we define

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{Q}_{t,e}

\displaystyle\equiv\tilde{Q}_{t}\left(g_{t}^{\ast},\ldots,g_{T}^{\ast}\right)-\tilde{Q}_{t}\left(\hat{g}_{t,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right),

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{Q}_{t,e}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\equiv\tilde{Q}_{t}\left(g_{t}^{\ast},\hat{g}_{t+1,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)-\tilde{Q}_{t}\left(\hat{g}_{t,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right),

\displaystyle\check{Q}_{nt}\left(g_{t},\ldots,g_{T}\right)

\displaystyle\equiv E_{n}\left[\hat{q}_{t}\left(Z,g_{t},\ldots,g_{T}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=t}^{T}E_{n}\left[\frac{(\prod_{\ell=t}^{s}1\left\{g_{\ell}\left(H_{\ell}\right)=D_{\ell}\right\})\gamma_{s}Y_{s}}{\prod_{\ell=t}^{s}\hat{e}_{\ell}\left(D_{\ell},H_{\ell}\right)}\right],

where
\tilde{Q}
is defined in (
16
).
The following lemmas will be used in the proof of Theorem
5.1
(ii).

Lemma F.5
.

Suppose that Assumptions

2.1
,
2.2
, and
2.4
hold for any
P\in\mathcal{P}(M,\kappa,\mathcal{G})
, that Assumption
2.3
holds for
\mathcal{G}
, that Assumption
3.1
holds for a pair
(P,\mathcal{G})
with any
P\in\mathcal{P}(M,\kappa,\mathcal{G})
, and that Assumption
5.1
(ii) holds for any
P\in\mathcal{P}_{e}
. Then, for any
P\in\mathcal{P}(M,\kappa,\mathcal{G})\cap\mathcal{P}_{e}
, the following hold:

(i)

for
t=1,\ldots,T
,

\displaystyle E_{P^{n}}\left[\Delta\tilde{Q}_{t,e}^{\dagger}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\sum_{s=t}^{T}\frac{\gamma_{s}M_{s}}{\prod_{\ell=t}^{s}\kappa_{\ell}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{s=t}^{T}v_{s}}{n}}+O\left(\xi_{n}^{-1}\right),

where
C
is the same constant term as introduced in Lemma
A.2
;

(ii)

for
t=1,\ldots,T-1
and
s=t+1,\ldots,T
,

\displaystyle\tilde{Q}_{t}\left(g_{t}^{\ast},\ldots,g_{T}^{\ast}\right)-\tilde{Q}_{t}\left(g_{t}^{\ast},\ldots,g_{s}^{\ast},\hat{g}_{s+1,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\prod_{\ell=t}^{s}\kappa_{\ell}}\Delta\tilde{Q}_{s+1,e};

(iii)

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{Q}_{1,e}

\displaystyle\leq\Delta\tilde{Q}_{1,e}^{\dagger}+\sum_{s=1}^{T-1}\frac{2^{s-1}}{\prod_{t=1}^{s}\kappa_{t}}\Delta\tilde{Q}_{s+1,e}^{\dagger}.

Proof.

(i) It follows for any
\tilde{g}_{t}\in{\cal G}_{t}
that

\displaystyle\tilde{Q}_{t}\left(\tilde{g}_{t},\hat{g}_{t+1,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)-\tilde{Q}_{t}\left(\hat{g}_{t,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{Q}_{nt}\left(\tilde{g}_{t},\hat{g}_{t+1,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)-\check{Q}_{nt}\left(\tilde{g}_{t},\hat{g}_{t+1,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)-\tilde{Q}_{nt}\left(\hat{g}_{t,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)+\check{Q}_{nt}\left(\hat{g}_{t,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)

\displaystyle+\tilde{Q}_{t}\left(\tilde{g}_{t},\hat{g}_{t+1,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)-\tilde{Q}_{t}\left(\hat{g}_{t,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)+\tilde{Q}_{nt}\left(\hat{g}_{t,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)-\tilde{Q}_{nt}\left(\tilde{g}_{t},\hat{g}_{t+1,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{s=t}^{T}\sum_{\text{$\underline{d}$}_{t:s}\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{t-s+1}}\left[\left(\frac{1\left\{\underline{D}_{t:s}=\underline{d}_{t:s}\right\}\gamma_{s}Y_{s}}{\prod_{\ell=t}^{s}e_{\ell}\left(d_{\ell},H_{\ell}\right)}-\frac{1\left\{\underline{D}_{t:s}=\underline{d}_{t:s}\right\}\gamma_{s}Y_{s}}{\prod_{\ell=t}^{s}\hat{e}_{\ell}\left(d_{\ell},H_{\ell}\right)}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\times\left(1\left\{\tilde{g}_{t}(H_{it})=d_{t}\right\}-1\left\{\hat{g}_{t,e}^{B}\left(H_{it}\right)=d_{t}\right\}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\tilde{Q}_{t}\left(\tilde{g}_{t},\hat{g}_{t+1,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)-\tilde{Q}_{t}\left(\hat{g}_{t,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)+\tilde{Q}_{nt}\left(\tilde{g}_{t},\hat{g}_{t+1,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)-\tilde{Q}_{nt}\left(\hat{g}_{t,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\text{$\underline{d}$}_{t:T}\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{T-t+1}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\hat{\eta}_{t}^{-k}\left(\text{$\underline{d}$}_{t:T},H_{iT}\right)-\eta_{t}\left(\text{$\underline{d}$}_{t:T},H_{iT}\right)\right|\right)

\displaystyle+2\sup_{\left(g_{t},\ldots,g_{T}\right)\in{\cal G}_{t}\times\cdots\times{\cal G}_{T}}\left|\tilde{Q}_{nt}\left(g_{t},\ldots,g_{T}\right)-\tilde{Q}_{t}\left(g_{t},\ldots,g_{T}\right)\right|.

The first inequality follows from the fact that
\hat{g}_{t,e}^{B}
maximizes

\check{Q}_{nt}\left(\cdot,\hat{g}_{t+1,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)
over
{\cal G}_{t}
.

Then we have

\displaystyle E_{P^{n}}\left[\Delta\tilde{Q}_{t,e}^{\dagger}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2E_{P^{n}}\left[\sup_{\left(g_{t},\ldots,g_{T}\right)\in{\cal G}_{t}\times\cdots\times{\cal G}_{T}}\left|\tilde{Q}_{nt}\left(g_{t},\ldots,g_{T}\right)-\tilde{Q}_{t}\left(g_{t},\ldots,g_{T}\right)\right|\right]

\displaystyle+\sum_{\text{$\underline{d}$}_{t:T}\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{T-t+1}}E_{P^{n}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\hat{\eta}_{t}^{-k}\left(\text{$\underline{d}$}_{t:T},H_{iT}\right)-\eta_{t}\left(\text{$\underline{d}$}_{t:T},H_{iT}\right)\right|\right]

Therefore, applying Lemma
A.2
to the first term in the right hand side (as in the proof of Lemma
A.3
(i)) and Assumption
5.1
(ii) to the second term in the right hand side leads to the result.

(ii) The proof of Lemma
F.5
follows from the same argument with the proof of Lemma
A.3
(ii).

(iii) We follow the same strategy as in Lemma
A.3
(iii). First, note that

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T,e}=\tilde{Q}_{T}\left(g_{T}^{\ast}\right)-\tilde{Q}_{T}\left(\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)=\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T,e}^{\dagger}.

Then, for
t=T-1
, we have

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T-1,e}

\displaystyle=\tilde{Q}_{T-1}\left(g_{T-1}^{\ast},g_{T}^{\ast}\right)-\tilde{Q}_{T-1}\left(\hat{g}_{T-1,e}^{B},\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{Q}_{T-1}\left(g_{T-1}^{\ast},g_{T}^{\ast}\right)-\tilde{Q}_{T-1}\left(g_{T-1}^{\ast},\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)+\tilde{Q}_{T-1}\left(g_{T-1}^{\ast},\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)-\tilde{Q}_{T-1}\left(\hat{g}_{T-1,e}^{B},\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\kappa_{T-1}}\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T,e}^{\dagger}+\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T-1,e}^{\dagger},

where the inequality follows from Lemma
F.5
(ii).

Generally, for any
k=1,\ldots,T-1
, it follows that

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T-k,e}

\displaystyle=\tilde{Q}_{T-k}\left(g_{T-k}^{\ast},\ldots,g_{T}^{\ast}\right)-\tilde{Q}_{T-k}\left(\hat{g}_{T-k,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=T-k}^{T}\left[\tilde{Q}_{T-k}\left(g_{T-k}^{\ast},\ldots,g_{s}^{\ast},\hat{g}_{s+1,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)-\tilde{Q}_{T-k}\left(g_{T-k}^{\ast},\ldots,g_{s-1}^{\ast},\hat{g}_{s,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=T-k}^{T}\left[\tilde{Q}_{T-k}\left(g_{T-k}^{\ast},\ldots,g_{T}^{\ast}\right)-\tilde{Q}_{T-k}\left(g_{T-k}^{\ast},\ldots,g_{s-1}^{\ast},\hat{g}_{s,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=T-k+1}^{T}\left[\tilde{Q}_{T-k}\left(g_{T-k}^{\ast},\ldots,g_{T}^{\ast}\right)-\tilde{Q}_{T-k}\left(g_{T-k}^{\ast},\ldots,g_{s-1}^{\ast},\hat{g}_{s,e}^{B},\ldots,\hat{g}_{T,e}^{B}\right)\right]+\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T-k,e}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=T-k+1}^{T}\frac{1}{\prod_{\ell=T-k}^{s-1}\kappa_{\ell}}\Delta\tilde{Q}_{s,e}+\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T-k,e}^{\dagger},

where the second line follows by taking a telescope sum;
the third line follows from the fact that
\left(g_{s+1}^{\ast},\ldots,g_{T}^{\ast}\right)

maximizes
\tilde{Q}_{T-k}\left(g_{T-k}^{\ast},\ldots,g_{s}^{\ast},\cdot,\ldots,\cdot\right)

over
{\cal G}_{s+1}\times\cdots\times{\cal G}_{T}
under Assumption

3.1
; the last line follows from Lemma
F.5
(ii).

Then, recursively, the following hold:

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T-1,e}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\kappa_{T-1}}\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T,e}+\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T-1,e}^{\dagger}=\frac{1}{\kappa_{T-1}}\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T,e}^{\dagger}+\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T-1,e}^{\dagger},

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T-2,e}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\kappa_{T-2}}\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T-1,e}+\frac{1}{\kappa_{T-2}\kappa_{T-1}}\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T,e}+\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T-2,e}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\kappa_{T-2}\kappa_{T-1}}\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T,e}^{\dagger}+\frac{1}{\kappa_{T-2}}\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T-1,e}^{\dagger}+\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T-2,e}^{\dagger},

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T-k,e}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=1}^{k}\frac{2^{k-s}}{\prod_{t=T-k}^{T-s}\kappa_{t}}\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T-s+1,e}^{\dagger}+\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T-k,e}^{\dagger}.

Therefore, when
k=T-1
, we have

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{Q}_{1,e}

\displaystyle\leq\Delta\tilde{Q}_{1,e}^{\dagger}+\sum_{s=1}^{T-1}\frac{2^{T-1-s}}{\prod_{t=1}^{T-s}\kappa_{t}}\Delta\tilde{Q}_{T-s+1,e}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\Delta\tilde{Q}_{1,e}^{\dagger}+\sum_{s=1}^{T-1}\frac{2^{s-1}}{\prod_{t=1}^{s}\kappa_{t}}\Delta\tilde{Q}_{s+1,e}^{\dagger}.

∎

Proof of Theorem
5.1
(ii).

Let
P\in\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{e}\bigcap{\cal P}\left(M,\kappa,\mathcal{G}\right)
be fixed. By the same argument as in the proof of Theorem
3.6

(ii), it follows for
g^{*}\in\mathop{\rm arg\penalty\ max}\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}}W(g)
that

\displaystyle W_{\mathcal{G}}^{\ast}-W(\hat{g}_{e}^{B})

\displaystyle=\tilde{Q}_{1}(g^{\ast})-\tilde{Q}_{1}(\hat{g}_{e}^{B})\leq\Delta\tilde{Q}_{1,e}

\displaystyle\leq\Delta\tilde{Q}_{1,e}^{\dagger}+\sum_{s=1}^{T-1}\frac{2^{s-1}}{\prod_{t=1}^{s}\kappa_{t}}\Delta\tilde{Q}_{s+1,e}^{\dagger},

where the second inequality follows from Lemma
F.5
(iii). Thus, since
W_{\mathcal{G}}^{\ast}-W(\hat{g}_{e}^{B})\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle E_{P^{n}}\left[W_{{\cal G}}^{\ast}-W\left(\hat{g}_{e}^{B}\right)\right]\leq E_{P^{n}}\left[\Delta\tilde{Q}_{1,e}^{\dagger}\right]+\sum_{s=1}^{T-1}\frac{2^{s-1}}{\prod_{t=1}^{s}\kappa_{t}}E_{P^{n}}\left[\Delta\tilde{Q}_{s+1,e}^{\dagger}\right].

Applying Lemma
F.5
(i) to each term in the right hand side gives

\displaystyle E_{P^{n}}\left[W_{{\cal G}}^{\ast}-W\left(\hat{g}_{e}^{B}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\frac{\gamma_{t}M_{t}}{\prod_{s=1}^{t}\kappa_{s}}\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{s=1}^{t}v_{s}}{n}}\right\}

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=2}^{T}\frac{2^{t-2}}{\prod_{s=1}^{t-1}\kappa_{s}}\left(C\sum_{s=t}^{T}\left\{\frac{\gamma_{s}M_{s}}{\prod_{\ell=t}^{s}\kappa_{\ell}}\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{\ell=t}^{s}v_{\ell}}{n}}\right\}\right)+O\left(\xi_{n}^{-1}\right).

Since this upper bound does not depend on
P\in\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{e}\bigcap{\cal P}\left(M,\kappa,\mathcal{G}\right)
,
the upper bound is uniform over
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{e}\bigcap{\cal P}\left(M,\kappa,\mathcal{G}\right)
.
∎

F.4
Proof of Theorem
C.1

This section provides the proof of Theorem
C.1
for the rank-dependent SWF (
27
). The following is a preliminary lemma.

Lemma F.6
.

(Lemma A.5 in
Kitagawa and
Tetenov 2021
)
Let
\mathcal{F}
be a class of uniformly bounded functions, that is, there exists
\bar{F}<\infty
such that
||f||_{\infty}\leq\bar{F}
for all
f\in\mathcal{F}
. Assume that
\mathcal{F}
is a VC-subgraph class with VC-dimension
v<\infty
. Let
(Y,Z)\sim P
, where
Y\geq 0
is a scalar (
Y
and
Z
may be dependent). Let
\{(Y_{i},Z_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}\sim P^{n}
be an iid sample from
P
. Assume that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{P(Y>y)}dy\leq M.

Then, there is a universal constant
C
such that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}E_{P^{n}}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f(Z_{i})1\{Y_{i}>y\}-E_{P}[f(Z)1\{Y>y\}]\right|\right]dy\leq C\bar{F}M\sqrt{\frac{v}{n}}.

holds for all
n\geq 1
.

Proof of Theorem
C.1
.

By the same argument as in the proof of Theorem
3.6
(i), it follows that

\displaystyle E_{P^{n}}\left[\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}W_{\Lambda}(g)-W_{\Lambda}(\hat{g}^{S})\right]\leq 2E_{P^{n}}\left[\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\left|\widehat{W}_{\Lambda}(g)-W_{\Lambda}(g)\right|\right].

(35)

Since
\Lambda(\cdot)
is convex and nonincreasing,

\displaystyle\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\left|\widehat{W}_{\Lambda}(G)-W_{\Lambda}(g)\right|

\displaystyle=\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}\Lambda\left(\widehat{F}_{g}(y)\vee 0\right)dy-\int_{0}^{\infty}\Lambda\left(F_{g}(y)\right)dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left|\Lambda\left(\widehat{F}_{g}(y)\vee 0\right)-\Lambda\left(F_{g}(y)\right)\right|dy

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\left|\Lambda\left(\widehat{F}_{g}(y)\vee 0\right)-\Lambda\left(F_{g}(y)\right)\right|dy

\displaystyle\leq\left|\Lambda^{\prime}(0)\right|\int_{0}^{\infty}\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\left|\widehat{F}_{g}(y)-F_{g}(y)\right|dy.

(36)

Combining (
35
) and (
36
) yields

\displaystyle E_{P^{n}}\left[\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}W_{\Lambda}(g)-W_{\Lambda}(\hat{g}^{S})\right]\leq 2\left|\Lambda^{\prime}(0)\right|\int_{0}^{\infty}E_{P^{n}}\left[\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\left|\widehat{F}_{g}(y)-F_{g}(y)\right|\right]dy.

For
g\in\mathcal{G}
, let

\displaystyle w_{g}(Z_{i})\equiv\frac{\prod_{t=1}^{T}1\{g_{t}(H_{it})=D_{it}\}}{\prod_{t=1}^{T}e_{t}\left(D_{it},H_{it}\right)}.

By Lemma
A.1
, the class of functions
\mathcal{W}=\{w_{g}(\cdot):g\in\mathcal{G}\}
is a VC-subclass with VC-dimension of at most
\sum_{t=1}^{T}v_{t}
. Assumption
2.4
implies that
w_{g}(Z_{i})\in\left[0,1/\left(\prod_{t=1}^{T}\kappa_{t}\right)\right]
; hence, functions in
\mathcal{W}
are uniformly bounded by
1/\left(\prod_{t=1}^{T}\kappa_{t}\right)
.

Since
F_{g}(y)=1-E_{P}\left[w_{g}(Z)\cdot 1\left\{\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{t}>y\right\}\right]
, under Assumption
2.1
, and
\widehat{F}_{g}(y)=1-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{g}(Z_{i})\cdot 1\{\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{t}>y\}
,

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{F}_{g}(y)-F_{g}(y)\right|=\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{g}(Z_{i})\cdot 1\left\{\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{t}>y\right\}-E_{P}\left[w_{g}(Z)\cdot 1\left\{\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{t}>y\right\}\right]\right|.

It follows from Assumption
28
that

\displaystyle\mathop{\text{\Large$\int_{\text{\normalsize$\scriptstyle\kern-1.63885pt0$}}^{\text{\normalsize$\scriptstyle\infty$}}$}}\nolimits\sqrt{P\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{t}>y\right)}dy\leq\max_{\underline{d}_{T}\in\{0,1\}^{T}}\mathop{\text{\Large$\int_{\text{\normalsize$\scriptstyle\kern-1.63885pt0$}}^{\text{\normalsize$\scriptstyle\infty$}}$}}\nolimits\sqrt{P\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{t}\left(\underline{d}_{t}\right)>y\right)}dy\leq\Upsilon.

Applying Lemma
F.6
to (
36
) yields

\displaystyle E_{P^{n}}\left[\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}W_{\Lambda}(g)-W_{\Lambda}(\hat{g}^{S})\right]\leq 2C\left|\Lambda^{\prime}(0)\right|\frac{\Upsilon}{\prod_{t=1}^{T}\kappa_{t}}\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{t=1}^{T}v_{t}}{n}}.

∎

F.5
Proofs of Theorems
E.1
and
E.3

The following lemma, which directly follows from Lemma 2 in
Zhou
et al. 2023
and its proof, plays important roles in the proofs of Theorems
E.1
and
E.3
.

Lemma F.7
.

Fix
t\in\{1,\ldots,T\}
. For any
\underline{d}_{t}\in\{0,1\}^{t}
, let
\{\Gamma_{i}(\underline{d}_{t})\}_{i=1}^{n}
be i.i.d. random variables with bounded supports. For any
\underline{g}_{t:T}\in\mathcal{G}_{t:T}
, let
\widetilde{Q}(\underline{g}_{t:T})\equiv\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}^{{\dagger}}(\underline{g}_{t:T})
, where
\Gamma_{i}^{{\dagger}}(\underline{g}_{t:T})\equiv\Gamma_{i}^{{\dagger}}((g_{t}(H_{it}),\ldots,g_{T}(H_{iT})))
, and
Q(\underline{g}_{t:T})\equiv E[\widetilde{Q}(\underline{g}_{t:T})]
. For any
\underline{g}_{t:T}^{a},\underline{g}_{t:T}^{b}\in\mathcal{G}_{t:T}
, denote
\widetilde{\Delta}(\underline{g}_{t:T}^{a},\underline{g}_{t:T}^{b})\equiv\widetilde{Q}(\underline{g}_{t:T}^{a})-\widetilde{Q}(\underline{g}_{t:T}^{b})
and
\Delta(\underline{g}_{t:T}^{a},\underline{g}_{t:T}^{b})\equiv Q(\underline{g}_{t:T}^{a})-Q(\underline{g}_{t:T}^{b})
. Let
\kappa(\cdot)
denote the entorpy integral defined in
Zhou
et al. 2023
. Then, under Assumption
2.3
, the following holds: For any
\delta\in(0,1)
, with probability at least
1-2\delta
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\underline{g}_{t:T}^{a},\underline{g}_{t:T}^{b}\in\mathcal{G}_{t:T}}\left|\widetilde{\Delta}(\underline{g}_{t:T}^{a},\underline{g}_{t:T}^{b})-\Delta(\underline{g}_{t:T}^{a},\underline{g}_{t:T}^{b})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left(54.4\sqrt{2}\kappa(\underline{g}_{t:T})+435.2+\sqrt{2\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{V_{t:T}^{\ast}}{n}}

\displaystyle+o\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right),

where
V_{t:T}^{\ast}\equiv\sup_{\underline{g}_{t:T}^{a},\underline{g}_{t:T}^{b}\in\mathcal{G}_{t:T}}E\left[\left(\Gamma_{i}^{{\dagger}}(\underline{g}_{t:T}^{a})-\Gamma_{i}^{{\dagger}}(\underline{g}_{t:T}^{b})\right)^{2}\right].

Proof of Theorem
E.1
.

Let us define

\displaystyle\widetilde{V}_{i}(g)=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\psi_{it}\left(\underline{g}_{t}\right)Y_{it}-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\psi_{it}\left(g\right)Q_{t}^{\underline{g}_{(t+1):T}}\left(H_{it},D_{i,}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\psi_{i,t-1}\left(\underline{g}_{t-1}\right)Q_{t}^{\underline{g}_{(t+1):T}}\left(H_{it},g_{t}(H_{it})\right),

\displaystyle\widehat{V}_{i}(g)=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\hat{\psi}_{it}^{-k(i)}\left(\underline{g}_{t}\right)Y_{it}-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\hat{\psi}_{it}^{-k(i)}\left(g\right)\widehat{Q}_{t}^{\underline{g}_{(t+1):T},-k(i)}\left(H_{it},D_{i,}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\hat{\psi}_{i,t-1}^{-k(i)}\left(\underline{g}_{t-1}\right)\widehat{Q}_{t}^{\underline{g}_{(t+1):T},-k(i)}\left(H_{it},g_{t}(H_{it})\right),

with

\psi_{it}\left(\underline{g}_{t}\right)\equiv\frac{\prod_{s=1}^{t}1\{D_{is}=g_{s}(H_{is})\}}{\prod_{s=1}^{t}e_{t}(H_{is},g_{s}(H_{is}))}.

We also define
V(g)=E\left[\widetilde{V}_{i}(g)\right]
. Lemma B.2 in
Sakaguchi 2024
shows that
V(g)=W(g)
under Assumptions
2.1
.

For any
g^{a},g^{b}\in\mathcal{G}
, we define

\displaystyle\Delta(g^{a},g^{b})

\displaystyle\equiv V(g^{a})-V(g^{b}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}(g^{a},g^{b})

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{V}_{i}(g^{a})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{V}_{i}(g^{b}),

\displaystyle\widehat{\Delta}(g^{a},g^{b})

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{V}_{i}(g^{a})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{V}_{i}(g^{b}).

Let
g_{opt}^{\ast}\in\mathop{\rm arg\penalty\ max}\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}}W(g)
. A standard argument of the statistical learning theory gives

\displaystyle W_{\mathcal{G}}^{\ast}-W\left(\hat{g}^{AIPW}\right)

\displaystyle=\Delta(g_{opt}^{\ast},\hat{g}^{AIPW})

\displaystyle\leq\Delta(g_{opt}^{\ast},\hat{g}^{AIPW})-\widehat{\Delta}(g_{opt}^{\ast},\hat{g}^{AIPW})

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{g^{a},g^{b}\in\mathcal{G}}\left|\Delta(g^{a},g^{b})-\widehat{\Delta}(g^{a},g^{b})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{g^{a},g^{b}\in\mathcal{G}}\left|\Delta(g^{a},g^{b})-\widetilde{\Delta}(g^{a},g^{b})\right|+\sup_{g^{a},g^{b}\in\mathcal{G}}\left|\widehat{\Delta}(g^{a},g^{b})-\widetilde{\Delta}(g^{a},g^{b})\right|,

(37)

where the first inequality follows because
\hat{g}^{AIPW}
maximizes
\widehat{V}(g)
over
\mathcal{G}
; hence,
\widehat{\Delta}(g^{\ast},\hat{g}^{AIPW})\leq 0
.

We can now evaluate
W_{\mathcal{G}}^{\ast}-W(\hat{g}^{AIPW})
through evaluating
\sup_{g^{a},g^{b}\in\mathcal{G}}\left|\Delta(g^{a},g^{b})-\widetilde{\Delta}(g^{a},g^{b})\right|
and
\sup_{g^{a},g^{b}\in\mathcal{G}}\left|\widehat{\Delta}(g^{a},g^{b})-\widetilde{\Delta}(g^{a},g^{b})\right|
. As for the former, under Assumptions
2.1
–
2.4
, we can apply Lemma
F.7
to obtain the following result: For any stage
t
and
\delta\in(0,1)
, with probability at least
1-2\delta
,

\displaystyle\sup_{g^{a},g^{b}\in\mathcal{G}}\left|\Delta(g^{a},g^{b})-\widetilde{\Delta}(g^{a},g^{b})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left(54.4\sqrt{2}\kappa(\mathcal{G})+435.2+\sqrt{2\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{V^{\ast}}{n}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right),

(38)

where

V^{\ast}\equiv\sup_{g^{a},g^{b}\in\mathcal{G}}E\left[\left(\widetilde{V}_{i}(g^{a})-\widetilde{V}_{i}(g^{b})\right)^{2}\right]<\infty.

Note that
\kappa(\mathcal{G})\leq 2.5\sqrt{VC(\mathcal{G})}<\infty
from Remark 8 in
Zhou
et al. 2023
and Assumption
2.3
.

As for the latter
\sup_{g^{a},g^{b}\in\mathcal{G}}\left|\widehat{\Delta}(g^{a},g^{b})-\widetilde{\Delta}(g^{a},g^{b})\right|
, under Assumptions
2.1
–
2.4
and
E.1
, we can obtain from Lemma A.3 in
Sakaguchi 2024
that

\displaystyle\sup_{g^{a},g^{b}\in\mathcal{G}}\left|\widehat{\Delta}(g^{a},g^{b})-\widetilde{\Delta}(g^{a},g^{b})\right|=O_{p}\left(n^{-\min\{1/2,\tau\}}\right).

(39)

Combining (
37
), (
38
), and (
39
) leads to the result.
∎

Proof of Lemma
E.2
.

Under Assumption
E.2
and the redefinition
H_{t}(\underline{d}_{t})=(\underline{D}_{t-1},\underline{X}_{t})
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{Y}_{t}\left(\underline{g}_{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\underline{d}_{t}\in\{0,1\}^{t}}Y_{t}(\underline{d}_{t})\cdot\prod_{s=1}^{t}1\left\{g_{s}\left(\underline{d}_{s-1},\underline{X}_{s}\right)=d_{s}\right\},

where we use the fact that
\underline{X}_{t}=\underline{X}_{t}(\underline{d}_{t})
for any
\underline{d}_{t}
. Then we have

\displaystyle W_{t}\left(\underline{g}_{t}\right)=\sum_{\underline{d}_{t}\in\{0,1\}^{t}}E_{P}\left[\gamma_{t}Y_{t}(\underline{d}_{t})\cdot\prod_{s=1}^{t}1\left\{g_{s}\left(\underline{d}_{s-1},\underline{X}_{s}\right)=d_{s}\right\}\right].

It follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{d}_{t}\in\{0,1\}^{t}}E_{P}\left[\mu_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{t})\cdot\prod_{s=1}^{t}1\{d_{s}=g_{s}(\underline{d}_{s-1},\underline{X}_{s})\}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\underline{d}_{t}\in\{0,1\}^{t}}E_{P}\left[E_{P}\left[\gamma_{t}Y_{t}(\underline{d}_{t})\cdot\prod_{s=1}^{t}1\{d_{s}=g_{s}(\underline{d}_{s-1},\underline{X}_{s})\}\middle|\underline{D}_{t}=\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{t}\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\underline{d}_{t}\in\{0,1\}^{t}}E_{P}\left[E_{P}\left[\gamma_{t}Y_{t}(\underline{d}_{t})\cdot\prod_{s=1}^{t}1\{d_{s}=g_{s}(\underline{d}_{s-1},\underline{X}_{s})\}\middle|\underline{D}_{t-1}=\underline{d}_{t-1},\underline{X}_{t}\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\underline{d}_{t}\in\{0,1\}^{t}}E_{P}\left[E_{P}\left[\gamma_{t}Y_{t}(\underline{d}_{t})\cdot\prod_{s=1}^{t}1\{d_{s}=g_{s}(\underline{d}_{s-1},\underline{X}_{s})\}\middle|\underline{D}_{t-1}=\underline{d}_{t-1},\underline{X}_{t-1}\right]\right].

where the second equality follows from Assumption
2.1
and the third follows by the law of iterated expectations. Applying the same argument recursively,

\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{d}_{t}\in\{0,1\}^{t}}E_{P}\left[\mu_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{t})\cdot\prod_{s=1}^{t}1\{d_{s}=g_{s}(\underline{d}_{s-1},\underline{X}_{s})\}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\underline{d}_{t}\in\{0,1\}^{t}}E_{P}\left[\gamma_{t}Y_{t}(\underline{d}_{t})\cdot\prod_{s=1}^{t}1\{d_{s}=g_{s}(\underline{d}_{s-1},\underline{X}_{s})\}\right]=W_{t}\left(\underline{g}_{t}\right).

∎

Proof of Theorem
E.3
.

For any
g\in\mathcal{G}
, let
\tilde{g}=(\tilde{g}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{g}_{T})
, where
\tilde{g}_{t}:\underline{\mathcal{X}}_{t}\rightarrow\{0,1\}
, be recursively defined as

\displaystyle\tilde{g}_{1}(x_{1})

\displaystyle=g_{1}(x_{1}),

\displaystyle\tilde{g}_{2}(\underline{x}_{2})

\displaystyle=g_{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(x_{1}),\underline{x}_{2}),

\displaystyle\tilde{g}_{3}(\underline{x}_{3})

\displaystyle=g_{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(x_{1}),\tilde{g}_{2}(\underline{x}_{2}),\underline{x}_{3}),

\displaystyle\tilde{g}_{t}(\underline{x}_{t})

\displaystyle=g_{t}(\tilde{g}_{1}(x_{1}),\tilde{g}_{2}(\underline{x}_{2}),\ldots,\tilde{g}_{t-1}(\underline{x}_{t-1}),\underline{x}_{t}),

\displaystyle\tilde{g}_{T}(\underline{x}_{T})

\displaystyle=g_{T}(\tilde{g}_{1}(x_{1}),\tilde{g}_{2}(\underline{x}_{2}),\ldots,\tilde{g}_{T-1}(\underline{x}_{T-1}),\underline{x}_{T}).

For the class
\mathcal{G}
of DTRs, we define the class of
\tilde{g}
as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}\equiv\left\{\tilde{g}=(\tilde{g}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{g}_{T}):g\in\mathcal{G}\right\}.

Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}:=\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1}\times\cdots\times\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{t}
. We denote by
\kappa(\cdot)
the entropy integral defined in
Zhou
et al. 2023
. Note that
\kappa(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\leq\kappa(\mathcal{G})
.

Without loss of generality, we suppose that
\gamma_{1}=\gamma_{2}=\cdots=\gamma_{T}
. Let us define, for
t=1,\ldots,T
,

\displaystyle{\Gamma}_{it}(\underline{d}_{t})

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{Y_{it}-{\mu}_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}{\eta_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}\cdot 1\{\underline{D}_{it}=\underline{d}_{t}\}+{\mu}_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it}),

\displaystyle\widehat{\Gamma}_{it}(\underline{d}_{t})

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{Y_{it}-\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k(i)}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}{\hat{\eta}_{t}^{-k(i)}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}\cdot 1\{\underline{D}_{it}=\underline{d}_{t}\}+\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k(i)}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it}),

where
\eta_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{x}_{t})\equiv\prod_{s=1}^{t}e_{s}(d_{s},H_{is})
and
\hat{\eta}_{t}^{-k(i)}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{x}_{t})\equiv\prod_{s=1}^{t}\hat{e}_{s}^{-k(i)}(d_{s},H_{is})
.

Let
\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}(\underline{x}_{t})=(\tilde{g}_{1}(x_{1}),\tilde{g}_{2}(\underline{x}_{2}),\ldots,\tilde{g}_{t}(\underline{x}_{t}))
. Given a fixed DTR
\tilde{g}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}
constructed from
g\in\mathcal{G}
, with some abuse of the notation, we define for
t=1,\ldots,T
,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{it}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t})

\displaystyle\equiv\sum_{\underline{d}_{t}\in\{0,1\}^{t}}{\Gamma}_{it}(\underline{d}_{t})\cdot 1\{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}(\underline{x}_{t})=\underline{d}_{t}\},

\displaystyle\widehat{\Gamma}_{it}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t})

\displaystyle\equiv\sum_{\underline{d}_{t}\in\{0,1\}^{t}}\widehat{\Gamma}_{it}(\underline{d}_{t})\cdot 1\{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}(\underline{x}_{t})=\underline{d}_{t}\}.

Note that
(1/n)\sum_{t=1}^{t}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\Gamma}_{it}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t})=\widehat{W}^{DR}(g)
. Hence,

\max_{\tilde{g}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{t=1}^{t}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\Gamma}_{it}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t})=\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\widehat{W}^{DR}(g).

Note also that
(1/n)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{it}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}\right)
is an oracle estimate of
W_{t}(\underline{g}_{t})
with oracle access to
\{\mu_{s}(\cdot,\cdot):s=1,\ldots,t\}
and
\{e_{s}(\cdot,\cdot):s=1,\ldots,t\}
.
For
\underline{g}_{t}\in\mathcal{G}_{1:t}
, we define
\widetilde{W}_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t})\equiv E\left[\Gamma_{it}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t})\right]
. Note that
W_{t}(\underline{g}_{t})=\widetilde{W}_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t})
by Lemma
E.2
under Assumptions
2.1
and
E.2
.

Following the analysis of
Zhou
et al. 2023
, we define the policy value difference function
\Delta_{t}(\cdot;\cdot):\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, the oracle influence difference function
\widetilde{\Delta}_{t}(\cdot;\cdot):\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, and the estimated policy value difference function
\widehat{\Delta}_{t}(\cdot;\cdot):\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, respectively, as follows: For
\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a}=(\tilde{g}_{1}^{a},\ldots,\tilde{g}_{t}^{a})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}
and
\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}=(\tilde{g}_{1}^{b},\ldots,\tilde{g}_{t}^{b})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})\equiv\widetilde{W}_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a})-\widetilde{W}_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})\equiv\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{it}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a}\right)-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{it}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\right),

\displaystyle\widehat{\Delta}_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})\equiv\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\Gamma}_{it}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a}\right)-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\Gamma}_{it}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\right).

Note that
\widetilde{\Delta}_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})
is an unbiased estimator of the policy value difference function
\Delta_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})
. From the definitions,

\displaystyle W_{\mathcal{G}}^{\ast}-W(\hat{g}^{DR})=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Delta_{t}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{1:t}^{\ast};\tilde{\hat{\underline{g}}}_{1:t}^{DR}\right).

A standard argument of the statistical learning theory gives

\displaystyle W_{\mathcal{G}}^{\ast}-W(\hat{g}^{DR})

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Delta_{t}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{1:t}^{\ast};\tilde{\hat{\underline{g}}}_{1:t}^{DR}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Delta_{t}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{1:t}^{\ast};\tilde{\hat{\underline{g}}}_{1:t}^{DR}\right)-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{\Delta}_{t}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{1:t}^{\ast};\tilde{\hat{\underline{g}}}_{1:t}^{DR}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}|\Delta_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})-\widehat{\Delta}_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}|\Delta_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})-\widetilde{\Delta}_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})|

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}|\widehat{\Delta}_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})-\widetilde{\Delta}_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})|,

(40)

where the first inequality follows because
\tilde{\hat{g}}^{DR}
maximizes
(1/n)\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\Gamma}_{it}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{1:t}\right)
over
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}
; hence,
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{\Delta}_{t}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{1:t}^{\ast};\tilde{\hat{\underline{g}}}_{1:t}^{DR}\right)\leq 0
.

We can now evaluate
W_{\mathcal{G}}^{\ast}-W(\hat{g}^{DR})
through evaluating
\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}|\Delta_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})-\widetilde{\Delta}_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})|
and
\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}|\widehat{\Delta}_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})-\widetilde{\Delta}_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})|
for each
t
. As for the former, under Assumptions
2.1
–
2.4
, we can apply Lemma
F.7
for the oracle influence difference function with some modifications to the notations to obtain the following result: For any stage
t
and
\delta\in(0,1)
, with probability at least
1-2\delta
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}\left|\widetilde{\Delta}_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})-\Delta_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left(54.4\sqrt{2}\kappa(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t})+435.2+\sqrt{2\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{V_{t}^{\ast}}{n}}

\displaystyle+o\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right),

(41)

where

V_{t}^{\ast}\equiv\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}E\left[\left(\Gamma_{it}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a})-\Gamma_{it}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})\right)^{2}\right]<\infty.

Note that from Remark 8 in
Zhou
et al. 2023
,
\kappa(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t})\leq 2.5\sqrt{VC(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t})}
. Using Lemma
A.1
and the fact that
VC(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t})\leq VC(\mathcal{G}_{1:t})
, it follwos that

\displaystyle\kappa(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t})\leq 2.5\sqrt{VC(\mathcal{G}_{1:t})}\leq 2.5\sum_{s=1}^{t}v_{s}<\infty,

(42)

where the last inequality follows from Assumption
2.3
.

We next consider evaluating
\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}|\widehat{\Delta}_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})-\widetilde{\Delta}_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})|
. We employ the general strategy of the poof of
Zhou
et al. 2023
(
Zhou
et al. 2023
, Lemma 3).
Fix
t
. For any
\underline{d}_{t}\in\{0,1\}^{t}
, let

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}_{t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})\equiv\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{it}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a}\right)-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{it}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\right),

\displaystyle\widehat{\Delta}_{t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})\equiv\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\Gamma}_{it}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a}\right)-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\Gamma}_{it}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\right),

with
\Gamma_{it}^{\underline{d}_{t}}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t})\equiv{\Gamma}_{it}(\underline{d}_{t})\cdot 1\{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}(\underline{x}_{t})=\underline{d}_{t}\}
and
\widehat{\Gamma}_{it}^{\underline{d}_{t}}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t})\equiv\sum_{\underline{d}_{t}\in\{0,1\}^{t}}\widehat{\Gamma}_{it}(\underline{d}_{t})\cdot 1\{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}(\underline{x}_{t})=\underline{d}_{t}\}
.
Noting that
\widehat{\Delta}_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})-\widetilde{\Delta}_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})=\sum_{\underline{d}_{t}\in\{0,1\}^{t}}\left(\widehat{\Delta}_{t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})-\widetilde{\Delta}_{t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})\right)
, we will provide an upper bound for each
\widehat{\Delta}_{t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})-\widetilde{\Delta}_{t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})
. To do so, we make the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\widehat{\Delta}_{t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})-\widetilde{\Delta}_{t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})=S_{1,t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})+S_{2,t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})+S_{3,t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}),

where

\displaystyle S_{(A1),t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}G_{i,\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left(\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k(i)}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})-{\mu}_{t}^{-k(i)}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})\right)\left(1-\frac{1\{\underline{D}_{it}=\underline{d}_{t}\}}{{\eta}_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}\right),

\displaystyle S_{(A2),t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}G_{i,\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left(Y_{it}-\mu_{t}^{-k(i)}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})\right)\left(\frac{1\{\underline{D}_{it}=\underline{d}_{t}\}}{\hat{\eta}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}-\frac{1\{\underline{D}_{it}=\underline{d}_{t}\}}{{\eta}_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}\right),

\displaystyle S_{(A3),t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}G_{i,\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left({\mu}_{t}^{-k(i)}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})-\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k(i)}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})\right)\left(\frac{1\{\underline{D}_{it}=\underline{d}_{t}\}}{\hat{\eta}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}-\frac{1\{\underline{D}_{it}=\underline{d}_{t}\}}{{\eta}_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}\right),

with
G_{i,\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}}^{\underline{d}_{t}}:=1\{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a}(\underline{X}_{it})=\underline{d}_{t}\}-1\{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}(\underline{X}_{it})=\underline{d}_{t}\}
.

For each fold
k
, define

\displaystyle S_{(A1),t}^{\underline{d}_{t},k}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\{i|k(i)=k\}}G_{i,\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left(\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})-{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})\right)\left(1-\frac{1\{\underline{D}_{it}=\underline{d}_{t}\}}{{\eta}_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}\right);

\displaystyle S_{(A2),t}^{\underline{d}_{t},k}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\{i|k(i)=k\}}G_{i,\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left(Y_{it}-\mu_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})\right)\left(\frac{1\{\underline{D}_{it}=\underline{d}_{t}\}}{\hat{\eta}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}-\frac{1\{\underline{D}_{it}=\underline{d}_{t}\}}{{\eta}_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}\right).

Note that
S_{(A1),t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})=\sum_{k=1}^{K}S_{(A1),t}^{\underline{d}_{t},k}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})
and
S_{(A2),t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})=\sum_{k=1}^{K}S_{(A2),t}^{\underline{d}_{t},k}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})
.

Fix
k\in\{1,\ldots,K\}
. We first consider
S_{(A1),t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})
. Since
\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\cdot)
is computed using the data in the rest
K-1
folds, when the data
\{Z_{i}:k(i)\neq k\}
in the rest
K-1
folds is conditioned,
\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\cdot)
is fixed; hence,
\widetilde{S}_{(A1),t}^{\underline{d}_{t},k}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\right)
is a sum of i.i.d. bounded random variables under Assumptions
2.2
,
2.4
, and
E.3
(ii).

It follows that

\displaystyle E\left[G_{i,\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left(\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})-{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})\right)\left(1-\frac{1\{\underline{D}_{it}=\underline{d}_{t}\}}{{\eta}_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}\right)\middle|\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\cdot)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\left[G_{i,\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left(\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})-{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})\right)E\left[\left(1-\frac{1\{\underline{D}_{it}=\underline{d}_{t}\}}{{\eta}_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}\right)\middle|\underline{X}_{it}\right]\middle|\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\cdot)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

where the last line follows from the sequential independence assumption (Assumption
2.1
).
Hence,
\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}\left|,k\widetilde{S}_{(A1),t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\right)\right|
can be written as

\displaystyle\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}\left|\widetilde{S}_{(A1),t}^{\underline{d}_{t},k}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{K}\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}\left|\frac{1}{n/K}\sum_{i\in I_{k}}\left\{G_{i,\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left(\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})-{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})\right)\left(1-\frac{1\{\underline{D}_{it}=\underline{d}_{t}\}}{{\eta}_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.-E\left[G_{i,\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left(\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})-{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})\right)\left(1-\frac{1\{\underline{D}_{it}=\underline{d}_{t}\}}{{\eta}_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}\right)\middle|\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\cdot)\right]\right\}\right|.

By applying Lemma
F.7
with setting
i\in I_{k}
and

\displaystyle\Gamma_{i}(d_{t})=G_{i,\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left(\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})-{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})\right)\left(1-\frac{1\{\underline{D}_{it}=\underline{d}_{t}\}}{{\eta}_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}\right),

the following holds:
\forall\delta>0
, with probability at least
1-2\delta
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}\left|\widetilde{S}_{(A1),t}^{\underline{d}_{t},k}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq o\left(n^{-1/2}\right)+\left(54.4\kappa\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}\right)+435.2+\sqrt{2\log(1/\delta)}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left[\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}E\left[\left(G_{i,\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\right)^{2}\left(\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})-{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})\right)^{2}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\left.\times\left(1-\frac{1\{\underline{D}_{it}=\underline{d}_{t}\}}{{\eta}_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}\right)\right|\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k}\left(\underline{d}_{t},\cdot\right)\right]\middle/\left(\frac{n}{K}\right)\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq o\left(n^{-1/2}\right)+\sqrt{K}\cdot\left(54.4\kappa\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}\right)+435.2+\sqrt{2\log(1/\delta)}\right)\cdot\left(1-\frac{1}{\eta}\right)^{t}

\displaystyle\times\sqrt{\frac{E\left[\left.\left(\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})-{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})\right)^{2}\right|\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k}\left(\underline{d}_{t},\cdot\right)\right]}{n}},

where the last inequality follows from
\left(G_{i,\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\right)^{2}\leq 1
a.s. and Assumption
2.4
(overlap condition). From Assumptions
2.2
and
E.3
(ii), we have
E\left[\left(\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})-{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})\right)^{2}\right]<\infty
. Hence, Markov’s inequality leads to

\displaystyle E\left[\left(\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})-{\mu}_{t}^{-k}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})\right)^{2}\middle|\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k}\left(\underline{d}_{t},\cdot\right)\right]=O_{p}(1).

Note also that
\kappa(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t})<\infty
from (
42
). Combining these results, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}\left|\widetilde{S}_{(A1),t}^{\underline{d}_{t},k}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\right)\right|=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

Consequently,

\displaystyle\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}\left|\widetilde{S}_{(A1),t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\right)\right|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}\left|\widetilde{S}_{(A1),t}^{\underline{d}_{t},k}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\right)\right|=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

(43)

By the same argument, we have
\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}\left|\widetilde{S}_{(A2),t}^{\underline{d}_{t},k}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\right)\right|=O_{p}\left(1/\sqrt{n}\right)
. Hence

\displaystyle\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}\left|\widetilde{S}_{(A2),t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\right)\right|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}\left|\widetilde{S}_{(A2),t}^{\underline{d}_{t},k}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\right)\right|=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

(44)

We next consider to bound
S_{(A3),t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}(\cdot,\cdot)
from above. It follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}\left|\widetilde{S}_{(A3),t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}G_{i,\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left({\mu}_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})-\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k(i)}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})\right)\left(\frac{1}{\hat{\eta}_{t}^{-k(i)}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}-\frac{1}{{\eta}_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\{i|\underline{D}_{it}=\underline{d}_{t}\}}\left|\left(\mu_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})-\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k(i)}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})\right)\right|\cdot\left|\left(\frac{1}{\hat{\eta}_{t}^{-k(i)}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}-\frac{1}{{\eta}_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\{i|\underline{D}_{it}=\underline{d}_{t}\}}\left(\mu_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})-\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k(i)}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})\right)^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\{i|\underline{D}_{it}=\underline{d}_{t}\}}\left(\frac{1}{\hat{\eta}_{t}^{-k(i)}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}-\frac{1}{{\eta}_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}\right)^{2}},

where the last inequality follows from Cauchy-Schwartz inequality. Taking expectation of both sides yields:

\displaystyle E\left[\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}\left|\widetilde{S}_{(A3),t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\right)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq E\left[\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\{i|\underline{D}_{it}=\underline{d}_{t}\}}\left(\mu_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})-\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k(i)}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})\right)^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\times E\left[\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\{i|\underline{D}_{it}=\underline{d}_{t}\}}\left(\frac{1}{\hat{\eta}_{t}^{-k(i)}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}-\frac{1}{{\eta}_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}\right)^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\{i|\underline{D}_{it}=\underline{d}_{t}\}}E\left[\left(\mu_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})-\hat{\mu}_{t}^{-k(i)}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})\right)^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\times\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\{i|\underline{D}_{it}=\underline{d}_{t}\}}E\left[\left(\frac{1}{\hat{\eta}_{t}^{-k(i)}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}-\frac{1}{{\eta}_{t}(\underline{d}_{t},\underline{X}_{it})}\right)^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle=o(n^{-\tau^{\prime}/2}),

where the second inequality follows from Cauchy-Schwartz inequality and the last line follows from Assumption
E.3
(i). Then applying Markov’s inequality leads to

\displaystyle\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}\left|\widetilde{S}_{(A3),t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(n^{-\tau^{\prime}/2}\right).

(45)

We therefore obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}|\widehat{\Delta}_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})-\widetilde{\Delta}_{t}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{\underline{d}_{t}\in\{0,1\}^{t}}\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}|\widehat{\Delta}_{t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})-\widetilde{\Delta}_{t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{\underline{d}_{t}\in\{0,1\}^{t}}\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}\left|\widetilde{S}_{(A1),t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\right)\right|+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{\underline{d}_{t}\in\{0,1\}^{t}}\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}\left|\widetilde{S}_{(A2),t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\right)\right|

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{\underline{d}_{t}\in\{0,1\}^{t}}\sup_{\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a},\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1:t}}\left|\widetilde{S}_{(A3),t}^{\underline{d}_{t}}\left(\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{a};\underline{\tilde{g}}_{t}^{b}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(n^{-\min\{1/2,\tau^{\prime}/2\}}\right),

(46)

where the last line follows from (
43
), (
44
), and (
45
).

Combining (
40
), (
41
), (
42
), and (
46
) leads to the result (
32
).
∎

Appendix G
Additional Simulation Results

We conduct an additional simulation study to examine the finite sample performance for the estimation methods proposed in Section
3
under the circumstance that the sequential independence assumption does not hold due to the presence of unobserved heterogeneity. We consider the same DGPs as those used in Section
6
, except that the treatment assignments
D_{1}
and
D_{2}
are distributed as

\displaystyle D_{1}\sim 1\{N(0,1)+\rho\cdot U_{1}\geq 0\}\mbox{ and }D_{2}\sim 1\{N(0,1)+\rho\cdot U_{2}\geq 0\}.

(47)

Recall that the potential outcomes
Y_{1}(d_{1})
and
Y_{2}(d_{1},d_{2})
depend on
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
, respectively. Hence, unless
\rho\neq 0
, the sequential independence assumption (Assumption
2.1
) is not satisfied. We consider two values of
\rho
:
\rho=-1
and
1
. For each
j=1,2,3
, we label the DGP that is the same as DGP
j
used in Section
6
except for that
D_{1}
and
D_{2}
follow equation (
47
) with
\rho=-1
and
1
as DGPs
j^{\prime}
and
j^{\prime\prime}
, respectively.

Table
4
presents the results of 500 simulations with sample sizes
n=200
,
500
, and
800
, where we compare Q-learning, backward DEWM, and simultaneous DEWM and calculate the mean and median welfare achieved by each estimated DTR.
Panel (A) of Table
4
shows that in the case of
\rho=-1
, simultaneous DEWM leads to the lower mean welfare in DGP
3^{\prime}
than Q-learning and backward DEWM. This result differs from the simulation results in Section
6
, where the DGPs satisfy the sequential independence assumption, and simultaneous DEWM leads to the highest mean welfare in DGP3. Panel (B) of Table
4
shows that the DGPs
1^{\prime\prime}
,
2^{\prime\prime}
, and
3^{\prime\prime}
lead to similar results to those with DGPs 1-3 in terms of the order of mean/median welfare among the three methods.

Appendix H
Computation

In this appendix, we explain computation of the backward and simultaneous DEWM with
\mathcal{G}_{t}
(
t=1,\ldots,T
) being classes of the linear treatment rules. The non-convexity of the objective functions make these computations challenging. However, the optimization problems can be formulated as Mixed Integer Linear Programming (MILP) problems, for which some efficient softwares (e.g., CPLEX; Gurobi) are available.
In the following subsections, we illustrate the MILP formalization for each of the backward and simultaneous DEWM in the case of
T=2
. We suppose that the class of feasible treatment rules for each stage
t=1,2
takes the form of
\mathcal{G}_{t}=\left\{1\left\{(1,H_{t}^{\prime})\beta_{t}\geq 0\right\}:\beta_{t}\in\mathcal{B}_{t}\subset\mathbb{R}^{(k+2)t-1}\right\}
where
\mathcal{B}_{t}
is a compact set.

H.1
Backward DEWM

Using slightly different notation from Section
3.1
, the first step of the backward DEWM method is

\displaystyle\max_{g_{2}\in{\cal G}_{2}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}m_{i2}^{B}g_{2},

where

\displaystyle m_{i2}^{B}=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{D_{i2}}{e_{2}\left(1,H_{i2}\right)}-\frac{1-D_{i2}}{e_{2}\left(0,H_{i2}\right)}\right)\gamma_{2}Y_{i2}.

Let
\hat{g}_{2}^{B}
be a maximizer of the above problem.
Then, the second step of the backward DEWM method is

\displaystyle\max_{g_{1}\in{\cal G}_{1}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}m_{i1}^{B}g_{1},

where

\displaystyle m_{i1}^{B}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{D_{i1}}{e_{1}\left(1,H_{i1}\right)}-\frac{1-D_{i1}}{e_{2}\left(0,H_{i1}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{D_{i2}\hat{g}_{2}^{B}\left(H_{i2}\right)}{e_{2}\left(1,H_{i2}\right)}-\frac{\left(1-D_{i2}\right)\left(1-\hat{g}_{2}^{B}\left(H_{i2}\right)\right)}{e_{2}\left(0,H_{i2}\right)}\right)\left(\gamma_{1}Y_{1i}+\gamma_{2}Y_{i2}\right).

When the class of DTRs is constrained to the class of linear eligibility
rules, each step of the backward DEWM method described in Section
3.1
can be formulated as MILP problem.
The optimization problem in the first step is equivalent to
the following MILP problem:

(First step)

\displaystyle\underset{\left(z_{12},\ldots,z_{n2}\right)\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{n}}{\max_{\beta_{2}\in\mathcal{B}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}m_{i2}^{B}z_{i2}

s.t.

\displaystyle\frac{(1,H_{i2}^{\prime})\beta_{2}}{C_{i2}}<z_{i2}\leq 1+\frac{(1,H_{i2}^{\prime})\beta_{2}}{C_{i2}}\ \mbox{for }i=1,\ldots,n,

where
C_{i2}
are constants that should satisfy
C_{i2}>\sup_{\beta_{2}\in\mathcal{B}_{2}}\left|(1,H_{i2}^{\prime})\beta_{2}\right|
.

Subsequently, the optimization problem in the second step is equivalent to
the following MILP problem:

(Second step)

\displaystyle\underset{\left(z_{11},\ldots,z_{n1}\right)\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{n}}{\max_{\beta_{1}\in\mathcal{B}_{1}}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}m_{i1}^{B}z_{i1}

s.t.

\displaystyle\frac{(1,H_{i1}^{\prime})\beta_{1}}{C_{i1}}<z_{i1}\leq 1+\frac{(1,H_{i1}^{\prime})\beta_{1}}{C_{i1}}\ \mbox{for }i=1,\ldots,n,

where
C_{i1}
are constants that should satisfy
C_{i1}>\sup_{\beta_{1}\in\mathcal{B}_{1}}\left|(1,H_{i1}^{\prime})\beta_{1}\right|
.

When we specify the dynamic treatment choice problem as the start (stop) time decision problem discussed in Section
2.2
, the linear constraints
z_{i2}\geq D_{i1}
and
D_{i2}\geq z_{i1}

(
z_{i2}\leq D_{i1}
and
D_{i2}\leq z_{i1}
) should be added into the MILP problems for the first and second steps, respectively. When we specify the problem as the one-shot treatment decision problem discussed in Section
2.2
, the linear constraints
z_{i2}+D_{i1}\leq 1
and
D_{i2}+z_{i1}\leq 1
should be added into the
MILP problems for the first and second steps, respectively.

H.2
Simultaneous DEWM

In the case of
T=2
, the optimization problem of the simultaneous DEWM method is equivalent to

\displaystyle\max_{\left(g_{1},g_{2}\right)\in{\cal G}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[m_{i1}^{S}g_{1}+m_{i2}^{S}g_{2}+m_{i3}^{S}g_{1}g_{2}\right],

where
m_{is}^{S}
for
s=1,2,3
are defined as

\displaystyle m_{i1}^{S}

\displaystyle=\frac{D_{i1}}{e_{1}\left(1,H_{i1}\right)}\left(\gamma_{1}Y_{i1}+\frac{\left(1-D_{i2}\right)\gamma_{2}Y_{i2}}{e_{2}\left(0,H_{i2}\right)}\right)-\frac{1-D_{i1}}{e_{1}\left(0,H_{i1}\right)}\left(\gamma_{1}Y_{i1}+\frac{\left(1-D_{i2}\right)\gamma_{2}Y_{i2}}{e_{2}\left(0,H_{i2}\right)}\right),

\displaystyle m_{i2}^{S}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\left(1-D_{i1}\right)D_{i2}}{e_{1}\left(0,H_{i1}\right)e_{2}\left(1,H_{i2}\right)}-\frac{\left(1-D_{i1}\right)\left(1-D_{i2}\right)}{e_{1}\left(0,H_{i1}\right)e_{2}\left(0,H_{i2}\right)}\right)\gamma_{2}Y_{i2},

\displaystyle m_{i3}^{S}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\left(d_{1},d_{2}\right)\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{2}}\frac{1\left\{D_{i1}=d_{1},D_{i2}=d_{2}\right\}\gamma_{2}Y_{i2}}{e_{1}\left(d_{1},H_{i1}\right)e_{2}\left(d_{2},H_{i2}\right)}.

When the class of DTRs is constrained to the class of linear eligibility
rules, the above optimization problem is equivalent to the following
MILP problem:

\displaystyle\underset{\left(z_{1t},\ldots,z_{nt}\right)_{t=1}^{3}\in\{0,1\}^{3n}}{\max_{\left(\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\right)\in\mathcal{B}_{1}\times\mathcal{B}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[m_{i1}^{S}z_{i1}+m_{i2}^{S}z_{i2}+m_{i3}^{S}z_{i3}\right]

s.t.

\displaystyle\frac{(1,H_{it}^{\prime})\beta_{t}}{C_{it}}<z_{it}\leq 1+\frac{(1,H_{it}^{\prime})\beta_{t}}{C_{it}}\ \mbox{for }i=1,\ldots,n\mbox{ and }t=1,2,

\displaystyle z_{i3}=z_{i1}z_{i2}\mbox{ for }i=1,\ldots,n,

where
C_{it}
are constants that should satisfy
C_{it}>\sup_{\beta_{t}\in\mathcal{B}_{t}}\left|(1,H_{it}^{\prime})\beta_{t}\right|
.

When we specify the dynamic treatment choice problem as the start (stop) time decision problem discussed in Section
2.2
, the linear constraints
z_{i2}\geq z_{i1}

(
z_{i2}\leq z_{i1}
) should be added into the MILP problem. When we specify the problem as the one-shot treatment decision problem discussed in Section
2.2
, the linear constraint
z_{i1}+z_{i2}\leq 1
should be added into the MILP problem.

H.3
Budget/Capacity Constraint

The budget/capacity constraints studied in Section
4
can be incorporated into the MILP problem for the simultaneous DEWM. The optimization
problem (
12
) with the class of linear eligibility score rules is formulated as the following MILP problem:

\displaystyle\underset{\left(z_{1t},\ldots,z_{nt}\right)_{t=1}^{3}\in\{0,1\}^{3n}}{\max_{\left(\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\right)\in\mathcal{B}_{1}\times\mathcal{B}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[m_{i1}^{S}z_{i1}+m_{i2}^{S}z_{i2}+m_{i3}^{S}z_{i3}\right]

s.t.

\displaystyle\frac{(1,H_{it}^{\prime})\beta_{t}}{C_{it}}<z_{it}\leq 1+\frac{(1,H_{it}^{\prime})\beta_{t}}{C_{it}}\ \mbox{for }i=1,\ldots,n\mbox{ and }t=1,2,

\displaystyle z_{i3}=z_{i1}z_{i2}\mbox{ for }i=1,\ldots,n,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{t=1}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}K_{tb}z_{it}\leq C_{b}+\alpha_{n}\ \mbox{for }b=1,\ldots,B\mbox{ and }t=1,2,

where
C_{it}
are constants that should satisfy
C_{it}>\sup_{\beta_{t}\in\mathcal{B}_{t}}\left|(1,H_{it}^{\prime})\beta_{t}\right|
.
The linear constraints in the last line correspond to the budget/capacity constraints.
